=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer numberOfUsers representing the total number of users and an array events of size n x 3.
Each events[i] can be either of the following two types:

Message Event: ["MESSAGE", "timestamp_i", "mentions_string_i"]

This event indicates that a set of users was mentioned in a message at timestamp_i.
The mentions_string_i string can contain one of the following tokens:
		
id<number>: where <number> is an integer in range [0,numberOfUsers - 1]. There can be multiple ids separated by a single whitespace and may contain duplicates. This can mention even the offline users.
ALL: mentions all users.
HERE: mentions all online users.




Offline Event: ["OFFLINE", "timestamp_i", "id_i"]

This event indicates that the user id_i had become offline at timestamp_i for 60 time units. The user will automatically be online again at time timestamp_i + 60.



Return an array mentions where mentions[i] represents the number of mentions the user with id i has across all MESSAGE events.
All users are initially online, and if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp.
Note that a user can be mentioned multiple times in a single message event, and each mention should be counted separately.
 
Example 1:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["MESSAGE","10","id1 id0"],["OFFLINE","11","0"],["MESSAGE","71","HERE"]]
Output: [2,2]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id1 and id0 are mentioned. mentions = [1,1]
At timestamp 11, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 71, id0 comes back online and "HERE" is mentioned. mentions = [2,2]

Example 2:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["MESSAGE","10","id1 id0"],["OFFLINE","11","0"],["MESSAGE","12","ALL"]]
Output: [2,2]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id1 and id0 are mentioned. mentions = [1,1]
At timestamp 11, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 12, "ALL" is mentioned. This includes offline users, so both id0 and id1 are mentioned. mentions = [2,2]

Example 3:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["OFFLINE","10","0"],["MESSAGE","12","HERE"]]
Output: [0,1]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 12, "HERE" is mentioned. Because id0 is still offline, they will not be mentioned. mentions = [0,1]

 
Constraints:

1 <= numberOfUsers <= 100
1 <= events.length <= 100
events[i].length == 3
events[i][0] will be one of MESSAGE or OFFLINE.
1 <= int(events[i][1]) <= 10^5
The number of id<number> mentions in any "MESSAGE" event is between 1 and 100.
0 <= <number> <= numberOfUsers - 1
It is guaranteed that the user id referenced in the OFFLINE event is online at the time the event occurs.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Need maybe if a user has offline_until = t and current_time = t, expire before event. If we process event by event and call expire before each, okay. But if we sort and process all events at same timestamp, we might expire once before group. Simpler:
events.sort(key=lambda x: (int(x[1]), 0 if x[0]=="OFFLINE" else 1))
offline = [-1]*numberOfUsers
mentions = [0]*numberOfUsers
for typ, ts_str, data in events:
    ts = int(ts_str)
    # expire all users with offline[i] != -1 and offline[i] <= ts
    for i in range(numberOfUsers):
        if offline[i] != -1 and offline[i] <= ts:
            offline[i] = -1
    if typ == "OFFLINE":
        uid = int(data)
        offline[uid] = ts + 60
    else: # MESSAGE
        for token in data.split():
            if token == "ALL":
                for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                    mentions[i] += 1
            elif token == "HERE":
                for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                    if offline[i] == -1:
                        mentions[i] += 1
            else:
                uid = int(token[2:])
                mentions[uid] += 1
return mentions

Need maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t for user A and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for user A, and a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 with HERE. Good.

Need maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t for user A and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for user A,
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        # OFFLINE events must be processed before MESSAGE events at the same timestamp.
        events.sort(key=lambda e: (int(e[1]), 0 if e[0] == "OFFLINE" else 1))

        offline_until = [-1] * numberOfUsers
        mentions = [0] * numberOfUsers

        for typ, ts_str, data in events:
            ts = int(ts_str)

            # Bring users back online before processing any event at this timestamp.
            for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                if offline_until[i] != -1 and offline_until[i] <= ts:
                    offline_until[i] = -1

            if typ == "OFFLINE":
                uid = int(data)
                offline_until[uid] = ts + 60
            else:  # MESSAGE
                for token in data.split():
                    if token == "ALL":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            mentions[i] += 1
                    elif token == "HERE":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            if offline_until[i] == -1:
                                mentions[i] += 1
                    else:
                        uid = int(token[2:])
                        mentions[uid] += 1

        return mentions
```
